### Fine arts and crafts



The *[Sumazau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumazau)* dance performance of Papar Kadazan at the [Monsopiad Heritage Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monsopiad_Heritage_Village)

[Handicraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handicraft) and [souvenir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Souvenir) productions are part of the tourism products in Sabah. In addition, the Sabah Crafts Exotica programme has been held annually since 2011 in different small local museums.[^cite_note-466][^cite_note-467] Following the various initiatives by state government to encourage local entrepreneurs for state handicrafts, there were a total of 526 entrepreneurs in 2012 which increased to 1,483 in 2013 and 1,702 in 2014 with total sales value up from RM31 million to RM56 million.[^cite_note-468]



The [roundabout](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roundabout) in Tambunan with the sculpture of [Sompoton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sompoton), the main musical instrument of Sabah

Every ethnic groups in Sabah are known for their traditional music instruments,[^cite_note-469] the coastal people of Bajau, Brunei Malays, Bugis, Illanun, Kedayan and Suluks known for their [gendang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gendang), [kompang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rebana) and [kulintangan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kulintangan);[^cite_note-470] while the interior people such as the Dusun known with their [bungkau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bungkau), [sompoton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sompoton) and turali, the Lun Bawang/Lun Dayeh with their bass, the Kadazan with their tongkungon, the Murut with their tagunggak, the Rungus with their sundatang, tontog and turuding;[^cite_note-471][^cite_note-rungus_culture-472] [suling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Suling) is mostly played by all the interior ethnic groups of Kadazan-Dusun, Murut, Rungus and Lun Bawang/Lun Dayeh in the state.[^cite_note-473] Every ethnic groups also known for their traditional dances; both Kadazan-Dusun were known for their *[Sumazau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sumazau)* dance, the Murut with their *Magunatip*,[^cite_note-474] the Rungus with their *Mongigol Sumundai*,[^cite_note-rungus_culture-472] The Lun Bawang/Lun Dayeh with their *Alai Busak Baku*, the Brunei Malays with their *Adai-Adai*,[^cite_note-475] the West Coast Bajau with their *Limbai* and *Kuda Pasu*, the East Coast Bajau and Suluk with their *[Pangalay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pangalay)* (also known as *Daling-Daling* or *Mengalai*), Bisaya with their *Liliput* and the Cocos Malays with their *Dansa* and *Nona Mansaya* along with many other dances from other sub-ethnic groups.[^cite_note-476][^cite_note-477] Beside that, the state of Sabah is also known for [batik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysian_batik) production though the industry are still small than the major batik producer states in the east coast of Peninsular Malaysia.[^cite_note-478] The state batik has since been commercialised to enter the international market.[^cite_note-479]

### Cuisine

Main article: [Sabahan cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabahan_cuisine)



A swordfish *[hinava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinava)* served with [sandwich bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pullman_loaf)

Notable dishes in Sabah include the [Beaufort mee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beaufort_mee),[^cite_note-480][^cite_note-noodles-481] *[bosou](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bosou)*,[^cite_note-482] *[hinava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinava)*,[^cite_note-483] *[linopot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linopot)*,[^cite_note-484] [ngiu chap](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ngiu_chap),[^cite_note-Sabah_noodles_capital-485][^cite_note-noodles_of_Sabah-486] *[pinaasakan sada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinaasakan_sada)*,[^cite_note-487] *[mee tauhu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mee_tauhu)*,[^cite_note-Sabah_noodles_capital-485][^cite_note-noodles_of_Sabah-486] [sang nyuk mee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sang_nyuk_mee),[^cite_note-Sabah_noodles_capital-485][^cite_note-noodles_of_Sabah-486] Sipitang [satay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Satay),[^cite_note-488][^cite_note-489] [Tuaran mee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuaran_mee),[^cite_note-noodles-481][^cite_note-490] *[tuhau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuhau)*,[^cite_note-491] the *[bambangan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bambangan_\(condiment\))* condiment (*[mangifera pajang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mangifera_pajang)*),[^cite_note-492] the *[hinompuka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinompuka)* snack,[^cite_note-493] along with various *[sinalau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sinalau)* smoked meats,[^cite_note-494] *[pusas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pusas)* snacks and many others.[^cite_note-495][^cite_note-496] Apart from these, Sabah also features a number of snacks like *[amplang](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amplang)*, *[cincin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cincin)*, *[kelupis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kelupis)*, *[lidah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lidah)*, *roti kahwin*, [UFOs](31814.md) *[pinjaram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinjaram)* and Sandakan [tart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tart)[^cite_note-497] and dessert like [Batik cake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batik_cake),[^cite_note-498] *[lamban](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lamban)*, *nuba tingaa*, *[punjung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Punjung)*, *sinamu*, and Tuaran coconut pudding.[^cite_note-499] Every ethnic group has its own cuisine with different styles of preparing, cooking, serving and eating the food. Examples of Sabah-based companies promoting [dairy product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dairy_product) and state drinks are like the [Desa Cattle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desa_Cattle), [Tenom coffee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenom_coffee) and [Sabah Tea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_Tea).[^cite_note-500] The indigenous people features a number of [alcoholic drinks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alcoholic_drink) such as *[bahar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bahar_\(drink\))*, *kinomol*/*[lihing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lihing)*/*[tuak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuak)*, *[montoku](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montoku_\(drink\))*, *sagantang*, *sikat*, and *[tapai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapai_\(drink\))*;[^cite_note-501] with the state itself becoming the third-highest in alcohol consumption in the country after Kuala Lumpur and Sarawak.[^cite_note-502] The [English Tea House and Restaurant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_Tea_House_and_Restaurant) in Sandakan is another attraction promoting the [British tea culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tea_in_the_United_Kingdom). Other international shops and restaurants such as for Western food, Middle Eastern food, Bruneian food, Indonesian food, Filipino food, Japanese food, Korean food, Russian food, Taiwanese food, Thai food and Vietnamese food have their presence there. The increasing number of tourists on the purpose of [culinary tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culinary_tourism) has since raised the local awareness on the importance of local food to state tourism.[^cite_note-503]

### Portrayal in media



Extract from the title page of the British North Borneo Official Gazette (the *[British North Borneo Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_North_Borneo_Herald)*) of 16 April 1902

Much of the information of the territory was kept in the records of *[Journal of the Royal Asiatic Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Journal_of_the_Royal_Asiatic_Society)* (since 1820) and *[British North Borneo Herald](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_North_Borneo_Herald)* (since 1883). [Joseph Hatton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Hatton) published one of the earliest book titled *North Borneo – Explorations and Adventures in the Equator* (1886) based on the exploration notes leave by his son Frank Hatton, who served under the [North Borneo Chartered Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Borneo_Chartered_Company); his son was accidentally killed during his journey in Segama River on North Borneo.[^cite_note-504] Ada Pryer wrote a book about her life in North Borneo titled *A Decade in Borneo* (1894, re-issued 2001) as her husband, [William Pryer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Burgess_Pryer), also served for the North Borneo Chartered Company.[^cite_note-505] The earliest known footage of North Borneo is from three American movies by [Martin and Osa Johnson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martin_and_Osa_Johnson) titled *Jungle Adventures* (1921), *Jungle Depths of Borneo* (1937) and *Borneo* (1937).[^cite_note-506] Australian author [Wendy Law Suart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wendy_Law_Suart) lived in North Borneo capital between 1949 and 1953 and wrote a book titled *The Lingering Eye – Recollections of North Borneo* based on her experiences there.[^cite_note-507]



*[Three Came Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Came_Home)*, a 1950 Hollywood film based on the memoir of [Agnes Newton Keith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnes_Newton_Keith) life in Sandakan, North Borneo (present-day Sabah) during World War II

[^cite_note-Three_Came_Home-80]

English author K.G. Tregonning wrote a book about his travel to Jesselton from Singapore in a book titled *North Borneo* (1960).[^cite_note-508] Various other American films have been taken in the state, such as the *[Three Came Home](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Came_Home)* (1950), a [Hollywood](418334.md) film based on the memoir of [Agnes Newton Keith](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnes_Newton_Keith) in her book depicting the situation of World War II in Sandakan.[^cite_note-Three_Came_Home-80] Keith also wrote three other books about the state: *Land Below the Wind*, *White Man Returns* and *Beloved Exiles*. A Japanese film called *[Sandakan No. 8](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandakan_No._8)* (1974) directed by [Kei Kumai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kei_Kumai) tells the story of prostitution by *[Karayuki-san](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karayuki-san)* in Sandakan Japanese brothel based on the 1972 book *Sandakan Brothel No. 8: An Episode in the History of Lower-Class* by Yamazaki Tomoko.[^cite_note-509] In the [Earl Mac Rauch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earl_Mac_Rauch) novelisation of the American [*Buckaroo Banzai*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Adventures_of_Buckaroo_Banzai_Across_the_8th_Dimension) novel (Pocket Books, 1984; repr. 2001), as well in the DVD film, [Buckaroo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buckaroo_Banzai_\(character\))'s archenemy Hanoi Xan is said to have his secret base in Sabah, in a "relic city of caves".[^cite_note-510] *[Bat\*21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bat*21)* (1988), another American film depicting the [Vietnam War](32611.md), was shot at various locations in the suburbs north of Kota Kinabalu, including [Menggatal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menggatal), [Telipok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telipok), Kayu Madang and Lapasan.[^cite_note-511] Another English author, [Redmond O'Hanlon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Redmond_O'Hanlon), also wrote a book titled *Into the Heart of Borneo* (1984) about Borneo island.[^cite_note-512] While Sydney-based Australian author Lynette Ramsay Silver wrote two books about the history of Sabah: *Sandakan – A Conspiracy of Silence* (1998) and *Blood Brothers – Sabah and Australia 1942–1945* (2010). In early 2016, a "Roll of Honour" immortalising 2,479 British and Australian soldiers who died in Sabah during the World War II was presented by a British [Royal Artillery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Artillery) veteran to Sabah State Tourism, Culture and Environment Department; the roll lists a record of the identity of every [prisoner of war](25008.md) (POW) during the [Sandakan Death March](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sandakan_Death_March).[^cite_note-513] In 2017, an English woman named Mary Christina Lewin (Tina Rimmer), who had lived in North Borneo since 1949,[^cite_note-514] was given the 'Sabah Cultural Icon' as the first person to receive the award for her lifelong contribution to the people in the territory and her biggest role as educationist and artist who portrayed the life scene of North Borneo through her artworks.[^cite_note-515]

Following the beginning of Malaysian films in 1970s along with the foundation of Sabah Film Production, several local films have been produced and filmed in the state by the state production, including *Keluarga Si Comat* (1975) and *Hapuslah Air Matamu* (1976) (produced with a collaboration with Indonesian Film Production).[^cite_note-516] Abu Bakar Ellah (popularly known as Ampal) then became the leading artist of Sabah comedy film with his film *Orang Kita*.[^cite_note-517] In the present day, state-produced dramas and documentaries are usually aired either on [TVi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVi_\(Malaysia\)), [TV1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV1_\(Malaysia\)) or [TV2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TV2_\(Malaysia\)) while state musics aired on radios through Bayu FM, [Kupi-Kupi FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupi-Kupi_FM), Sabah FM and Sabah vFM. Sabah was featured in the British popular reality show *[Survivor: Borneo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Survivor:_Borneo)* and the American show *[Eco-Challenge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eco-Challenge) Borneo* in 2000.[^cite_note-518][^cite_note-519] In 2001, the state was featured in a 2001 Filipino documentary titled *Sabah: Ang Bagong Amerika?* by [Vicky Morales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vicky_Morales) on the story of Filipino immigrants from the Sulu Archipelago escaping [poverty and starvation in the Philippines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poverty_in_the_Philippines) by entering Sabah illegally to earn livehood but facing risk being caught, tortured and deported as Malaysian laws are getting strict on illegal migration.[^cite_note-520] In 2003, the state was featured on [*The Amazing Race*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Amazing_Race_4) for the first time as well on a 2009 Hong Kong drama of [*Born Rich*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Born_Rich_\(TV_series\)).[^cite_note-521] The state was also featured in a 2014 American documentary *[Sacred Planet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_Planet)* and featured again in a new edition of [*The Amazing Race*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Amazing_Race_24) as well on a Korean reality show programme titled the [*Law of the Jungle*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_of_the_Jungle_\(TV_program\)), both in 2014.[^cite_note-522] In early 2017, [Hong Kong film industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Hong_Kong) once again choose Sabah as one of the location for a new romance film titled *She Will Be Loved*.[^cite_note-523]

### Holidays and festivals

Main articles: [Public holidays in Sabah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Sabah) and [Public holidays in Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Malaysia)



During the harvest festival of *[Kaamatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaamatan)*, there is heritage beauty pageant of *[Unduk Ngadau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unduk_Ngadau)* as well as the *Mr. Kaamatan* being held annually at the [KDCA Compound](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KDCA_Compound) in Sabah to honour of the legendary *[Huminodun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huminodun)*, a maiden who sacrificed herself to save her people.

[^cite_note-524]

[^cite_note-525]



The Borneo Bug Fest in 2016, featuring [Volkswagen Beetle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volkswagen_Beetle)

Sabahans observe a number of holidays and festivals throughout the year.[^cite_note-526] Apart from the national [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Malaysia\)), [Malaysia Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia_Day) celebrations and the State Governor's birthday, Sabah celebrates [Sabah Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_Day) annually on 31 August.[^cite_note-527][^cite_note-528] Every ethnic groups celebrate their own festivals and the culture of open house (*rumah terbuka*) with the visits of families and friends from other races and religion are a norm especially with the interracial marriage between different ethnic groups of different background.[^cite_note-Ipgrave2008-529] Sabah are the only state in Malaysia to declare the [Kaamatan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kaamatan) celebration a public holiday.[^cite_note-530] Both Sabah and Sarawak are also the only two states in Malaysia that declare [Good Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Good_Friday) a public holiday.[^cite_note-Ipgrave2008-529][^cite_note-531] Many festivals are being held annually in Sabah such as the [Bon Odori](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bon_festival#Bon_Odori) Festival,[^cite_note-532] Sabah Jazz,[^cite_note-533] Borneo Bird Festival,[^cite_note-534] Borneo Bug Fest, Borneo Eco Film Festival,[^cite_note-535] Kota Kinabalu Food Fest,[^cite_note-536] Kota Kinabalu Jazz Festival,[^cite_note-537] Sabah Dragon Boat Festival, Sabah Fest,[^cite_note-538] Sabah International Folklore Festival and Sabah Sunset Music Festival.[^cite_note-539]

### Sports



The [Borneo International Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo_International_Marathon) in 2015

North Borneo sent its own teams to participate in the [1956](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Borneo_at_the_1956_Summer_Olympics) [Summer Olympic Games](27566.md),[^cite_note-540] [1958](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1958_British_Empire_and_Commonwealth_Games) and [1962 British Empire and Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_British_Empire_and_Commonwealth_Games),[^cite_note-541] as well on the [1962 Asian Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1962_Asian_Games) before its athletes started representing Malaysia after 1963.[^cite_note-542][^cite_note-543] To produce more athletes and to improve and raise the standard of sports in the state after Sabah became part of Malaysia, the Sabah State Sports Council was established in 1972.[^cite_note-544] In addition, the Sabah Sports and Cultural Board Sports was created on 1 September 1976 before being frozen in December 1978 for more than two years until 1 January 1981 due to specific reasons.[^cite_note-sabah_sports_board-545] On 31 December 1996, the board been split into Sport Authority of Sabah and Sabah Cultural Board with a new board been established as the Sabah Sports Board that was maintained until present.[^cite_note-sabah_sports_board-545] Sabah became the host of [SUKMA Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SUKMA_Games) in [2002](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002_Sukma_Games) and was crowned overall champions of the 2022 Para SUKMA Games.[^cite_note-546] The state also sends athletes to participate in the Borneo Games,[^cite_note-547] with some of them representing Malaysia at several international sporting events such as the [SEA Games](1575383.md), [Asian Games](39205.md), [Commonwealth Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commonwealth_Games) and the [Olympic Games](22576.md). Since 2003, the State Sports Council organises the Sabah Games (SAGA), a biennial state-level multi-sport event aimed at scouting young sports talent to represent the State at national sporting events. From an inter-zone event, the Games evolved into an inter-district event in 2009, with each of Sabah's districts rotate among themselves as host.[^cite_note-548] Sabah features various international sporting events, such as the annual [Borneo International Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo_International_Marathon) and [Mount Kinabalu International Climbathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Kinabalu_International_Climbathon). Besides focusing on main sports, Sabah also features eleven traditional sports.[^cite_note-549]



[Likas Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likas_Stadium), which is the home stadium for [Sabah F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_F.C._\(Malaysia\))

There are 12 sports complexs within the state with three main stadiums.[^cite_note-550] [Likas Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Likas_Stadium) is the main stadium for the state's football association, [Sabah F.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabah_F.C._\(Malaysia\)), followed by Penampang Stadium and Tawau Stadium. Sabah F.C. (formerly Sabah FA) was founded in 1963, having won one title each in the [Malaysia FA Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia_FA_Cup) in 1995, [Malaysia Premier League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malaysia_Premier_League) in [1996](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liga_Perdana_\(1994%E2%80%9397\)) and [2019](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Malaysia_Premier_League), [President Cup Malaysia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_Cup_Malaysia) in 1999, 13 titles in the past [Borneo Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borneo_Cup) and 11 titles in the women's football [Tun Sharifah Rodziah Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tun_Sharifah_Rodziah_Cup).[^cite_note-551][^cite_note-552] The association was returned to private sector in early 1996, which had long under the purview of the state government.[^cite_note-553] But following the argument between the association and Sabah Sports Board, Sabah FA was suspended by the state sports council on 15 January 1998 and the management was put under the [national sport ministry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Youth_and_Sports_\(Malaysia\)).[^cite_note-suspension-554] The move was seen as breaching [FIFA](11049.md) rules that stated there should be no government interference on football organisations.[^cite_note-suspension-554] The persistent problems plaguing the Sabah FA since 1980s have significantly deteriorating the team performances and demoralising players in addition to the scandals that have embroiling the Malaysian football in 1994.[^cite_note-555] In 2019, both Sabah and Sarawak Sports Ministries work together to establish the East Malaysia Sports Commission to facilitate the organisation of more sports programmes in the two territories including other places in the Borneo islands.[^cite_note-556] With the increase of youth interests on [e-sports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-sports), Sabah government also targeting to develop the sports as part of the tourism development in the state.[^cite_note-557][^cite_note-558][^cite_note-559][^cite_note-560]

## International relations

Sabah is a sister state/province to [Jiangxi Province](173817.md) in China,[^cite_note-561][^cite_note-562][^cite_note-563] [Ratchaburi Province](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratchaburi_Province) in Thailand[^cite_note-564] and [East Kalimantan](453651.md) in Indonesia.

[ISBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISBN_\(identifier\)) [978-0-945971-73-3](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:BookSources/978-0-945971-73-3). Since 1980, the Sabah Museum staff have carried out excavations in the Madai and Baturong limestone massifs, at caves and open sites dated back 30,000 years. Baturong is surrounded by large area of alluvial deposits, formed by the damming of the Tingkayu River by a lava flow. The Tingkayu stone industry shows a unique level of skills for its period. The remains of many mammals, snakes, and tortoises were found, all food items collected by early occupants of the rock shelters.

[^cite_note-37]: &#x20;Edward Gibbon (1788). ["Fall In The East — The Decline And Fall Of The Roman Empire \[Chapter 64\]"](https://www.ccel.org/g/gibbon/decline/volume2/chap64.htm). [Christian Classics Ethereal Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Classics_Ethereal_Library). [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20190716103253/http://www.ccel.org/g/gibbon/decline/volume2/chap64.htm) from the original on 16 July 2019. Retrieved 18 October 2017. Hundred thousand Chinese imitated his example; and the whole empire, from [Tonkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasions_of_Vietnam) to the [great wall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Wall_of_China), submitted to the dominion of [Cublai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kublai_Khan). His boundless ambition aspired to the conquest of [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_invasions_of_Japan): his fleet was twice shipwrecked; and the lives of a hundred thousand Mongols and Chinese were sacrificed in the fruitless expedition. But the neighbouring kingdoms, Corea, [Tonkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tonkin), [Cochinchina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cochinchina), [Pegu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pegu), [Bengal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bengal), and [Tibet](31516.md), were reduced in different degrees of tribute and obedience by the effort or terror of his arms. He explored the Indian Ocean with a fleet of a thousand ships: they sailed in sixty-eight days, most probably to Borneo, under the equinoctial line; and though they returned not without spoil or glory, the emperor was dissatisfied that the savage king had escaped from their hands.

[^cite_note-186]: &#x20;["Philippines rebel leader arrested"](https://news.bbc.co.uk/2/hi/asia-pacific/1673857.stm). *BBC News*. 25 November 2001. [Archived](https://web.archive.org/web/20150926005935/http://news.bbc.co.uk/2/hi/asia-pacific/1673857.stm) from the original on 26 September 2015. Retrieved 26 September 2015. Malaysia's Inspector-General of Police Norian Mai said Mr Misuari and six of his followers were arrested at 3.30 am on Saturday (1930 GMT Friday) on Jampiras island off Sabah state. Manila had ordered his arrest on charges of instigating a rebellion after the government suspended his governorship of an autonomous Muslim region in Mindanao, the ARMM. Although the Philippines has no extradition treaty with Malaysia, the authorities have already made clear that they intend to hand Mr Misuari over to the authorities in Manila as soon as possible. Malaysian Prime Minister Mahathir Mohamad had said before the arrest that, although his country had provided support to the rebel group in the past in its bid for autonomy, Mr Misuari had not used his powers correctly. "Therefore, we no longer feel responsible to provide him with any assistance," he said.
